The proposed framework enables researchers to systematically investigate how different models handle specific temporal dependencies, which is crucial for guiding architectural design and improving model performance. The work also validates existing theoretical insights and suggests new directions for theoretical investigation. The use of synthetic data and models also is extremely practical because it's very cost-effective. However, the theoretical value for the field would require this research is validated by many researchers/publications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined framework with clear methodology.
    *   Rigorous experiments with multiple architectures and settings.
    *   Confirmation of existing theoretical insights and discovery of new phenomena.
    *   Clear presentation of results and discussion of limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The focus on single-layer models limits the applicability of the findings to more complex, multi-layered architectures.
    *   The use of restricted memory function forms may not capture all types of temporal dependencies found in real-world data.
    *   The exclusive use of synthetic data, while allowing for better control, raises questions about the generalizability of the results to real-world datasets.

*   **Potential Influence:** The framework provides a valuable tool for researchers working on sequence modeling architectures. It could lead to a better understanding of the strengths and weaknesses of different models and guide the development of more efficient and effective architectures. The code provided as a part of the submission can allow easier reproducibility of experimental results, making it a stronger research contribution.

*   **Justification for Score:** Given the incremental novelty of the approach, the practical value of the framework, and the validation of existing theories, but mindful of the limitations associated with synthetic data and single-layer models, a score of 7 reflects a solid contribution to the field. It is not a breakthrough paper that fundamentally changes our understanding, but it provides a valuable tool and insights that could have a significant impact on the development of sequence models.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning Design-Score Manifold to Guide Diffusion Models for Offline Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05680v1)
- **Authors**: Tailin Zhou, Zhilin Chen, Wenlong Lyu, Zhitang Chen, Danny H. K. Tsang, Jun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Optimizing complex systems, from discovering therapeutic drugs to designing high-performance materials, remains a fundamental challenge across science and engineering, as the underlying rules are often unknown and costly to evaluate. Offline optimization aims to optimize designs for target scores using pre-collected datasets without system interaction. However, conventional approaches may fail beyond training data, predicting inaccurate scores and generating inferior designs. This paper introduces ManGO, a diffusion-based framework that learns the design-score manifold, capturing the design-score interdependencies holistically. Unlike existing methods that treat design and score spaces in isolation, ManGO unifies forward prediction and backward generation, attaining generalization beyond training data. Key to this is its derivative-free guidance for conditional generation, coupled with adaptive inference-time scaling that dynamically optimizes denoising paths. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that ManGO outperforms 24 single- and 10 multi-objective optimization methods across diverse domains, including synthetic tasks, robot control, material design, DNA sequence, and real-world engineering optimization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ManGO, a diffusion-based framework for offline optimization that learns a design-score manifold to guide diffusion models.  Unlike existing methods which treat design and score spaces separately, ManGO learns the interdependencies holistically, unifying forward prediction and backward generation. This allows for better generalization beyond the training data. The framework includes a derivative-free guidance mechanism for conditional generation and adaptive inference-time scaling which optimizes denoising paths. ManGO's performance is evaluated across various domains, including synthetic tasks, robot control, material design, DNA sequence, and real-world engineering, showing it outperforms existing single- and multi-objective optimization methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of learning the joint design-score manifold within a diffusion framework is a significant step forward.  Existing methods typically focus on either forward modeling (score prediction) or backward generation (design from score), but ManGO combines both effectively. The derivative-free guidance for conditional generation is also a valuable contribution, avoiding the limitations of forward models. The adaptive inference-time scaling further refines the generation process and enhances performance. However, diffusion models for generation are not new, and conditional diffusion has been explored before. The novelty lies in the specific application and the careful combination of techniques to create a robust offline optimization framework.

*   **Significance:** Offline optimization is a crucial area with applications spanning various scientific and engineering domains. ManGO's ability to effectively optimize designs using pre-collected datasets, without the need for costly online evaluations, is highly valuable.  The extensive experimental results across diverse benchmarks convincingly demonstrate the framework's superiority over existing methods. The ability to handle both single- and multi-objective optimization tasks adds to the significance. ManGO's potential to accelerate drug discovery, materials design, and other complex optimization problems is substantial. The visualization and analysis of the learned manifold and generation trajectories offer insights into the optimization process. However, the dependence on large datasets might limit its applicability in scenarios with scarce offline data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong theoretical foundation based on learning design-score manifold.
    *   Novel derivative-free guidance mechanism.
    *   Adaptive inference-time scaling for dynamic optimization.
    *   Comprehensive experimental validation across diverse domains.
    *   State-of-the-art performance compared to a wide range of baselines.
    *   Well-written and clear explanation of the methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   High dependence on the quality and quantity of offline data.
    *   The current implementation is limited to quasi-static environments.
    *   Lack of an iterative refinement mechanism post-generation.
    *   Computational cost of training and inference for large datasets may be high.
    *   The specific implementation of score-based reweighting could be further explored.

*   **Impact:**  ManGO has the potential to significantly impact the field of offline optimization by providing a more effective and versatile framework for design generation and optimization. The code and datasets being available will facilitate further research and adoption of the framework. The approach will likely inspire new research directions in manifold learning and conditional generative modeling for optimization.

**Justification:**

I am assigning a score of 8 because while the individual components (diffusion models, conditional generation) aren't entirely new, the unique combination of these components with the design-score manifold learning, derivative-free guidance, and adaptive inference-time scaling creates a genuinely novel and highly effective framework for offline optimization. The extensive empirical evidence, demonstrated across a diverse set of tasks and datasets, firmly establishes ManGO's superior performance compared to existing methods. The weaknesses, while important, don't diminish the core contributions and potential impact of the work.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Pts3D-LLM: Studying the Impact of Token Structure for 3D Scene Understanding With Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05689v1)
- **Authors**: Hugues Thomas, Chen Chen, Jian Zhang
- **Abstract**: Effectively representing 3D scenes for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) is crucial yet challenging. Existing approaches commonly only rely on 2D image features and use varied tokenization approaches. This work presents a rigorous study of 3D token structures, systematically comparing video-based and point-based representations while maintaining consistent model backbones and parameters. We propose a novel approach that enriches visual tokens by incorporating 3D point cloud features from a Sonata pretrained Point Transformer V3 encoder. Our experiments demonstrate that merging explicit 3D features significantly boosts performance. Furthermore, we show that point-based token structures can rival video-based ones when the points are cleverly sampled and ordered. Our best models from both structures achieve state-of-the-art results on multiple 3D understanding benchmarks. We emphasize our analysis of token structures as a key contribution, alongside transparent reporting of results averaged over multiple seeds, a practice we believe is vital for robust progress in the field.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Pts3D-LLM: Studying the Impact of Token Structure for 3D Scene Understanding With Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper "Pts3D-LLM" investigates the impact of different token structures on the performance of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) for 3D scene understanding. The authors focus on two types of token structures: video-based and point-based.  They propose a novel method for enriching visual tokens by incorporating 3D point cloud features extracted from a pre-trained Sonata encoder based on Point Transformer V3 architecture. They also introduce FPS6D, a view-sensitive point sampling strategy to create more informative point-based token sequences.  The authors maintain consistent model backbones and parameters to isolate the effect of the token structures.  Their experiments demonstrate that incorporating 3D features improves performance and that FPS6D-enhanced point-based structures can achieve state-of-the-art results on various 3D scene understanding benchmarks. The paper emphasizes the importance of token structure analysis and rigorous experimental methodology, including multi-seed averaging.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   *Strengths:* The paper presents several novel aspects. First, the method for enriching visual tokens with explicit 3D geometric features from a point cloud encoder represents a valuable contribution. This goes beyond simply relying on 2D image features, which has been a common limitation in previous work. Second, the FPS6D sampling strategy is a new and interesting approach to point cloud subsampling that takes into account viewpoint diversity. The rigorous and controlled comparison of different tokenization strategies, isolating the impact of token structure from other factors, is also novel and well-executed.
    *   *Weaknesses:* While the individual components are innovative, the overall architecture builds upon the Video-3D LLM framework. The core idea of combining visual and language modalities in an LLM is not entirely new. The use of pre-trained Sonata encoder and SigLIP also reduces the degree of novelty, as these are existing components. The token permutations are intuitive and, to some extent, incremental.

*   **Significance:**
    *   *Strengths:* The paper addresses a crucial challenge in 3D scene understanding with MLLMs: how to effectively represent 3D scenes as tokens. The findings that explicit 3D features boost performance and that point-based structures can rival video-based structures when properly designed have significant implications for future research. The state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The emphasis on rigorous experimentation, reproducibility, and multi-seed averaging is a valuable contribution to the field.
    *   *Weaknesses:* The reliance on pre-trained models (Sonata, SigLIP) limits the ability to understand the end-to-end learning process and potential bottlenecks. The analysis, while thorough, is primarily empirical. There is limited theoretical insight into *why* certain token structures work better than others. While the performance gains are significant, the overall impact may be constrained by the architectural dependence on Video-3D LLM and the use of existing pre-trained modules. The experiments are mainly on ScanNet, a somewhat limited dataset.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methods and experimental setup. The figures and tables are informative. The authors thoroughly discussed their methods and results.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in 3D scene understanding with MLLMs by highlighting the importance of token structure design and providing a new sampling strategy (FPS6D). The code release will facilitate further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign the paper a score of **7.5**. The paper presents significant novelty in the method of enriching visual tokens, the FPS6D sampling strategy, and the rigorous comparison of token structures. The state-of-the-art performance and emphasis on reproducibility enhance its significance. The primary weaknesses are the reliance on pre-trained models and existing architectures, the empirical nature of the analysis, and the limited datasets. While not a groundbreaking paradigm shift, the paper constitutes a solid and valuable contribution that advances the field.

### When to use Graphs in RAG: A Comprehensive Analysis for Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05690v1)
- **Authors**: Zhishang Xiang, Chuanjie Wu, Qinggang Zhang, Shengyuan Chen, Zijin Hong, Xiao Huang, Jinsong Su
- **Abstract**: Graph retrieval-augmented generation (GraphRAG) has emerged as a powerful paradigm for enhancing large language models (LLMs) with external knowledge. It leverages graphs to model the hierarchical structure between specific concepts, enabling more coherent and effective knowledge retrieval for accurate reasoning.Despite its conceptual promise, recent studies report that GraphRAG frequently underperforms vanilla RAG on many real-world tasks. This raises a critical question: Is GraphRAG really effective, and in which scenarios do graph structures provide measurable benefits for RAG systems? To address this, we propose GraphRAG-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark designed to evaluate GraphRAG models onboth hierarchical knowledge retrieval and deep contextual reasoning. GraphRAG-Bench features a comprehensive dataset with tasks of increasing difficulty, coveringfact retrieval, complex reasoning, contextual summarization, and creative generation, and a systematic evaluation across the entire pipeline, from graph constructionand knowledge retrieval to final generation. Leveraging this novel benchmark, we systematically investigate the conditions when GraphRAG surpasses traditional RAG and the underlying reasons for its success, offering guidelines for its practical application. All related resources and analyses are collected for the community at https://github.com/GraphRAG-Bench/GraphRAG-Benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the question of when and why Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation (GraphRAG) outperforms vanilla RAG, a crucial question given recent reports of GraphRAG's underperformance in real-world tasks despite its conceptual advantages. To answer this, the authors introduce GraphRAG-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark designed to evaluate GraphRAG models on hierarchical knowledge retrieval and deep contextual reasoning. GraphRAG-Bench includes datasets with varying information densities and tasks of increasing difficulty, covering fact retrieval, complex reasoning, contextual summarization, and creative generation. The authors perform a systematic evaluation across the entire pipeline, from graph construction to final generation, investigating the conditions for GraphRAG's success and offering guidelines for its practical application. They highlight the limitations of existing benchmarks and showcase scenarios where graph structures provide measurable benefits for RAG systems. The results confirm that basic RAG is appropriate for simple fact retrieval, while GraphRAG excells in complex reasoning across connected concepts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the development of GraphRAG-Bench, a new benchmark specifically designed to evaluate GraphRAG. Existing benchmarks are demonstrably inadequate for assessing GraphRAG's strengths due to their overemphasis on retrieval difficulty, limited task complexity, and reliance on generic corpora. GraphRAG-Bench addresses these shortcomings by incorporating a hybrid corpus (novels and medical guidelines) and task categories that require hierarchical reasoning and contextual synthesis.
    The idea of a benchmark for GraphRAG is not inherently novel, as others have explored specific improvements to RAG. However, the paper takes a more holistic evaluation approach, considering the complete pipeline from graph construction to answer generation. This makes the benchmark more valuable for understanding the nuances of GraphRAG performance.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the RAG literature. By identifying the limitations of current evaluation methodologies, it provides a more nuanced understanding of when GraphRAG offers a substantial benefit over traditional RAG. The findings are significant because they offer practical guidance for researchers and practitioners on when and how to effectively utilize GraphRAG. Furthermore, the benchmark itself serves as a valuable resource for the community, enabling more rigorous and comprehensive evaluations of GraphRAG models. The systematic evaluation, which takes into consideration the entire pipeline and also offers insights about limitations of graph based RAG, adds to the significance of this paper.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** GraphRAG-Bench is a well-designed and comprehensive benchmark that addresses the limitations of existing evaluation methodologies.
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough and systematic evaluation of GraphRAG models across different tasks and datasets.
    *   **Practical Guidelines:** The paper offers practical guidelines for when and how to effectively utilize GraphRAG, which are valuable for both researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Clear problem definition**: The paper begins by clearly defining the problem: the observed underperformance of GraphRAG models despite their theoretical advantages. This immediately establishes the relevance and motivation for the work.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on GPT-4**: The initial creation of the benchmark relied on GPT-4 for initial logic mining, question generation. Although the authors claim that this process is followed by manual checking and correction, a fully human generated benchmark would perhaps be more robust.
    *   **Limited Model Diversity:** While the evaluation includes representative GraphRAG frameworks, a more exhaustive comparison across a broader range of architectures could further strengthen the findings.
    *   **Efficiency Concerns**: While the benchmark acknowledges efficiency challenges, the analysis of computational costs and latency could be further expanded.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of RAG research by promoting more rigorous and comprehensive evaluation methodologies. GraphRAG-Bench will likely become a standard benchmark for evaluating GraphRAG models, driving further innovation in this area.
*   **Justification**: Despite minor weaknesses, the paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing a critical issue in the field, providing a comprehensive benchmark, and offering practical guidelines. The systematic analysis and benchmark also provide a solid foundation for future research in improving existing models.

### SafeGenBench: A Benchmark Framework for Security Vulnerability Detection in LLM-Generated Code
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05692v1)
- **Authors**: Xinghang Li, Jingzhe Ding, Chao Peng, Bing Zhao, Xiang Gao, Hongwan Gao, Xinchen Gu
- **Abstract**: The code generation capabilities of large language models(LLMs) have emerged as a critical dimension in evaluating their overall performance. However, prior research has largely overlooked the security risks inherent in the generated code. In this work, we introduce \benchmark, a benchmark specifically designed to assess the security of LLM-generated code. The dataset encompasses a wide range of common software development scenarios and vulnerability types. Building upon this benchmark, we develop an automatic evaluation framework that leverages both static application security testing(SAST) and LLM-based judging to assess the presence of security vulnerabilities in model-generated code. Through the empirical evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs on \benchmark, we reveal notable deficiencies in their ability to produce vulnerability-free code. Our findings highlight pressing challenges and offer actionable insights for future advancements in the secure code generation performance of LLMs. The data and code will be released soon.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**
The paper introduces SafeGenBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the security of code generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). The benchmark consists of a diverse set of 558 programming tasks designed to assess the susceptibility of LLM-generated code to common vulnerabilities, particularly those related to the OWASP Top-10 and CWE classifications. The paper also presents an automated evaluation framework that leverages both static application security testing (SAST) and LLM-based judging to identify vulnerabilities. The authors conduct experiments using several prominent LLMs, revealing security weaknesses in their generated code. They propose recommendations for improving the secure deployment of code-generation models.

**Critical Evaluation:**
*Novelty:* The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of a benchmark specifically designed for assessing the security of LLM-generated code. While previous benchmarks exist for evaluating code correctness and efficiency, SafeGenBench fills a gap by focusing on security vulnerabilities across a wide range of common software development scenarios. The dual-judge approach, combining SAST and LLM-based analysis, adds another layer of novelty to the evaluation framework. This combined method makes security evaluation far more detailed than with either tool. The comparative study contrasting SAST with the LLM judge and discussing both limitations and capabilities is also of high value.

*Significance:* The paper's significance stems from the increasing reliance on LLMs for code generation in real-world software development. The identification of security vulnerabilities in LLM-generated code raises serious concerns about the potential for introducing security flaws into software systems. By providing a benchmark and evaluation framework, the paper enables researchers and developers to systematically assess and improve the security of LLM-generated code. The findings highlight the need for better security alignment in LLM-based code generation systems. SafeGenBench can also be used to compare different LLMs and determine which generate the safest code. This benchmark gives the authors the ability to identify the key areas that need improvement in the field.
*Weaknesses:* The test cases used in SafeGenBench appear limited to single-function code generation tasks. The authors acknowledged that future work should expand the benchmark to project-level generation queries involving multi-step logic and interdependent modules. Since only security vulnerabilities in code generated by LLMs were analyzed and the generated code was not assessed for its ability to complete the intended task, future work should explore a more comprehensive evaluation framework that jointly considers both task completion and code security. It is unclear to what degree model understanding of the Chinese-language prompts affected their responses. The current judging process relies on a single LLM-based judge and one SAST tool, and a more diverse and comprehensive tool set might improve the judging accuracy.

*Potential Influence:* SafeGenBench has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in the field of LLM-based code generation. It can guide the development of more secure code generation models and inform best practices for secure deployment. It will enable future researchers to compare results on the same benchmark and more quickly test improvements. The benchmark can also be used to evaluate and compare commercial tools and ensure they are producing secured output.

The paper makes a significant contribution by filling a major void in the field. While there are some limitations, the work is of high value and will likely significantly influence the field.

### Being Strong Progressively! Enhancing Knowledge Distillation of Large Language Models through a Curriculum Learning Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05695v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyuan Liu, Mengxiang Zhang
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Distillation (KD) compresses large language models (LLMs) by transferring the teacher model's capabilities to a smaller student model, reducing inference cost and memory usage while maintaining performance. However, existing KD methods for LLMs often fail to prevent significant shifts in the student model's distribution during training, leading to issues such as catastrophic forgetting, mode collapse, and training-inference mismatch. To address these challenges, we propose a novel, plug-in curriculum learning framework inspired by the strength training principle of "progressive overload" (POCL), which can be seamlessly integrated into existing white-box KD approaches with minimal computational overhead. The framework comprises two core components: (1) a difficulty measurer that ranks and partitions training samples from easy to hard, and (2) a training scheduler that incrementally introduces these subsets into the distillation process at fixed intervals while applying loss functions with progressively rising temperatures. By starting with the easiest samples and progressively increasing the difficulty, the approach enhances both the stability and efficiency of learning. Extensive experiments in instruction-following settings demonstrate that POCL consistently improves the performance of distilled student models across various white-box KD methods and model families. Our findings highlight the effectiveness of sorted training samples in KD for LLMs. More generally, our work demonstrates how to structure training data within the KD process to enhance the stability and performance of distilled LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces POCL (Progressive Overload-Based Curriculum Learning), a novel plug-in curriculum learning framework designed to enhance knowledge distillation (KD) for large language models (LLMs). POCL aims to address issues like catastrophic forgetting and training-inference mismatch by structuring the training data within the KD process. It consists of two main components: (1) a difficulty measurer that ranks training samples from easy to hard using reciprocal rank fusion based on ROUGE-L scores and cross-entropy loss and (2) a training scheduler that incrementally introduces these subsets during distillation with progressively rising temperatures. The paper demonstrates that POCL consistently improves the performance of distilled student models across various white-box KD methods and model families.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in the application of the "progressive overload" principle, borrowed from strength training, to the curriculum learning framework for LLM knowledge distillation.  While curriculum learning itself isn't a new concept, its specific adaptation to KD in LLMs, with a focus on stabilizing the student model's distribution and mitigating training-inference mismatches, is a significant contribution. The difficulty measurer combining ROUGE-L and cross-entropy is a reasonable heuristic but not highly novel. The design of the training scheduler is also relatively straightforward.  The combination of these elements, however, into a cohesive framework targeted at the specific challenges of LLM KD, demonstrates novelty.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper comes from its practical impact and generalizability. The authors convincingly demonstrate that POCL consistently improves the performance of various white-box KD methods across different model families and datasets. This suggests that POCL can be readily integrated into existing KD pipelines to enhance the effectiveness of LLM distillation. The findings highlight the importance of structured training data, particularly when addressing capacity gaps between teacher and student models.  The paper also underscores the challenges of training-inference mismatch in LLM distillation, providing a concrete approach to mitigate this issue. However, it is worth mentioning that the relative improvement is sometimes marginal, and there is no theoretical analysis that supports its benefits, which is mostly an empirical demonstration.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in LLM KD and how POCL addresses them.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed and conducted, covering a range of KD methods, model families, and datasets. Ablation studies further dissect the contribution of each component of the POCL framework.
*   **Plug-in Nature:** The modularity of POCL makes it easy to adopt and integrate into existing KD workflows.
*   **Open-Source Code:** The availability of the code promotes reproducibility and encourages further research and development.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a rigorous theoretical analysis to support the effectiveness of POCL. A deeper understanding of the underlying mechanisms would strengthen the paper's contributions.
*   **Difficulty Measure Heuristic:** The combination of Rouge-L and cross entropy is not highly novel.
*   **Incremental Performance Improvement:** Although POCL consistently enhances the base KD methods, in several cases the performance gain is relatively limited, raising questions about its real-world impact.
*   **Limited Model Diversity:** Although several KD methods are tested, both teacher and student models remain in the same family (either GPT-2 or OPT).

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of 7 to this paper.

*   It introduces a novel application of curriculum learning to LLM knowledge distillation using a strength training concept.
*   The experimental results demonstrate that POCL consistently improves the performance of various KD methods.
*   The plug-in nature of POCL makes it easy to adopt and integrate into existing KD workflows.
*   However, the paper lacks a solid theoretical background.
*   Moreover, the actual impact of POCL is still somewhat limited. While the authors report consistent improvements, these increases are, in many cases, moderate, and the improvements might not be worth the extra complexity and implementation effort in all KD scenarios.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### RKEFino1: A Regulation Knowledge-Enhanced Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05700v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Wang, Yueru He, Ruoyu Xiang, Jeff Zhao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) hold great promise for financial applications but introduce critical accuracy and compliance challenges in Digital Regulatory Reporting (DRR). To address these issues, we propose RKEFino1, a regulation knowledge-enhanced financial reasoning model built upon Fino1, fine-tuned with domain knowledge from XBRL, CDM, and MOF. We formulate two QA tasks-knowledge-based and mathematical reasoning-and introduce a novel Numerical NER task covering financial entities in both sentences and tables. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization capacity of RKEFino1 in compliance-critical financial tasks. We have released our model on Hugging Face.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RKEFinol, a regulation knowledge-enhanced large language model (LLM) built upon the Fino1 model. RKEFinol is fine-tuned using data from XBRL, CDM, and MOF to improve its performance in Digital Regulatory Reporting (DRR) tasks. The authors formulate two question-answering tasks (knowledge-based and mathematical reasoning) and a numerical named entity recognition (NER) task to evaluate the model. Experimental results demonstrate that RKEFinol outperforms the baseline Fino1 model, showcasing improved accuracy and generalization capabilities in DRR-related financial tasks, especially in recognizing numerical entities within both textual and tabular formats. The authors release their model on Hugging Face.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper tackles the critical challenge of applying LLMs to Digital Regulatory Reporting (DRR), where accuracy and compliance are paramount. The risk of hallucinations and inaccuracies in LLMs makes their direct application to finance problematic.
    *   **Domain-Specific Fine-Tuning:** The authors fine-tune the model with curated domain knowledge from XBRL, CDM, and MOF, which is a sensible approach to improve performance in this specific domain.
    *   **Novel Task Formulation (Numerical NER):** The introduction of a numerical NER task that covers various financial entity types (Integer, Monetary, Percent, etc.) and processes data in both sentences and tables is a valuable contribution. It fills a gap in existing NER research which might focus solely on textual content or predefined tags.
    *   **Performance Improvement:** The experimental results show a clear improvement of RKEFinol over the baseline Fino1 model across different evaluation tasks, including the numerical NER task. This validates the effectiveness of the fine-tuning approach.
    *   **Release of Model:** The model is released on Hugging Face which promotes reproducibility and further development by the community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty (Model Architecture):** While the application to DRR is important, the core model architecture (fine-tuning Fino1) isn't fundamentally new. The primary novelty lies in the specific datasets and task formulation. RKEFinol essentially follows standard fine-tuning practices, which are not innovative by themselves.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The paper lacks detailed ablation studies. More analysis showing the individual impact of fine-tuning with XBRL vs. CDM vs. MOF data would strengthen the paper and provide more insight into which knowledge source is most beneficial for performance on each task.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While Accuracy and FactScore are relevant for QA, there is a gap for detailed evaluation of FactScore in different facets and context.
    *   **FactScore limitations**
        *   FactScore has a relatively high computational cost, as it relies on a separate NLI model to determine the consistency between generated answers and source documents.
        *  Some studies suggest that FactScore can be sensitive to certain variations in the generated text, such as paraphrasing or reordering of information, which can affect its accuracy.
    *   **Comparison to Existing Work:** The paper could be improved by including benchmarks against a wider range of existing finance-specific LLMs. It would be interesting to see how RKEFinol compares to models like BloombergGPT, FinGPT, or PIXIU when fine-tuned on the same datasets. Comparing the raw zero-shot performance of these models might also give a different perspective.
    *   **Limited Generalizability Discussion:** While the model's generalizability is assessed using the new NER task, more discussion on the limitations of the current approach and its ability to generalize to entirely new regulatory domains (beyond XBRL, CDM, and MOF) is needed.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper addresses a practical need in the financial industry by focusing on compliance and accuracy in LLM-driven DRR.
    *   The task formulation (especially numerical NER) can serve as a benchmark for future research in this area.
    *   The release of the model and datasets helps foster collaboration and accelerate progress in financial LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper has merit, addressing a critical issue and providing a useful contribution to the field of financial LLMs. However, its limited architectural novelty and lack of in-depth ablation studies prevent it from achieving a higher score. The task formulation, especially numerical NER, is a strong positive, but the overall approach is somewhat incremental. The performance gains are noteworthy, but the improvements could be more thoroughly analyzed with more ablation studies.

- **Score**: 6/10

### Token Transforming: A Unified and Training-Free Token Compression Framework for Vision Transformer Acceleration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05709v1)
- **Authors**: Fanhu Zeng, Deli Yu, Zhenglun Kong, Hao Tang
- **Abstract**: Vision transformers have been widely explored in various vision tasks. Due to heavy computational cost, much interest has aroused for compressing vision transformer dynamically in the aspect of tokens. Current methods mainly pay attention to token pruning or merging to reduce token numbers, in which tokens are compressed exclusively, causing great information loss and therefore post-training is inevitably required to recover the performance. In this paper, we rethink token reduction and unify the process as an explicit form of token matrix transformation, in which all existing methods are constructing special forms of matrices within the framework. Furthermore, we propose a many-to-many Token Transforming framework that serves as a generalization of all existing methods and reserves the most information, even enabling training-free acceleration. We conduct extensive experiments to validate our framework. Specifically, we reduce 40% FLOPs and accelerate DeiT-S by $\times$1.5 with marginal 0.1% accuracy drop. Furthermore, we extend the method to dense prediction tasks including segmentation, object detection, depth estimation, and language model generation. Results demonstrate that the proposed method consistently achieves substantial improvements, offering a better computation-performance trade-off, impressive budget reduction and inference acceleration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a unified and training-free token compression framework called "Token Transforming" for accelerating Vision Transformers (ViTs).  It reframes existing token reduction methods (pruning and merging) as special cases of a more general token matrix transformation.  The key idea is a "many-to-many" token transformation, which aims to preserve more information than exclusive pruning or merging approaches.  The framework consists of (1) an attentive-based token selection to identify informative tokens, (2) calculating a similarity matrix between original and informative tokens, and (3) using this matrix to transform the original tokens.  A token recovery module is introduced for dense prediction tasks.  Experiments demonstrate that this approach achieves comparable or better accuracy and speedup compared to existing methods, often without requiring fine-tuning.  The method is evaluated on image classification, semantic segmentation, object detection, depth estimation, and language model generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the unification of token pruning and merging under a generalized token transformation matrix framework, particularly the "many-to-many" aspect.  The idea of re-interpreting existing methods as specific instances of a more general mathematical operation has merit. While token pruning and merging strategies have been separately investigated, framing them as different instances of a matrix transformation provides a theoretical foundation and allows for a more unified view of token reduction. Additionally, the training-free aspect contributes to the practicality of the method. However, the individual components (attention-based token selection, similarity calculation using cosine distance) are not entirely novel on their own. The main contribution is the combination and the theoretical justification of this combination.

*   **Significance:** The paper has significant potential for practical impact.  ViT acceleration is a crucial research area, and a training-free method with minimal accuracy drop is highly desirable. The fact that the framework can be applied across multiple vision tasks (classification, segmentation, detection, depth estimation) and even to multimodal language models increases its value. If widely adopted, it could significantly reduce the computational costs associated with ViTs, making them more accessible for resource-constrained environments and applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Framework:** Provides a clean and understandable way to see token compression.
    *   **Training-Free:** Eliminates the need for resource-intensive fine-tuning.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** Consistently outperforms existing methods across a range of tasks, including dense prediction where training-free methods are often less successful.
    *   **Generalizability:** Demonstrates effectiveness on multiple ViT architectures and downstream tasks.
    *   **Practical Impact:** The focus on inference acceleration and reduced computational cost is highly relevant.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Components:**  While the framework is novel, the individual components (attention-based selection, cosine similarity) are relatively standard techniques. The paper could have benefited from a deeper theoretical analysis or exploration of alternative similarity metrics and token selection strategies.
    *   **Limited Ablation:** While ablation studies are included, more in-depth investigations of the hyperparameter settings are needed, specially about the effect of the number of remaining tokens. How does changing the number of preserved tokens affect the performance in different downstream tasks?
    *   **Scalability concerns**: How does the proposed Token Transforming framework handle very large-scale ViTs and datasets? Does the many-to-many transformation introduce significant computational overhead that diminishes its benefits at larger scales?
    *   **Dependence on Attention Maps:**  The method relies heavily on the attention maps. It would be helpful to know how performance degrades when the attention maps are not well-calibrated or reliable.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized. The figures are informative and help illustrate the proposed method. However, some of the ablation study results could be presented more clearly to emphasize the importance of each component.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to be highly influential if the method proves to be robust and widely applicable. It could become a standard technique for accelerating ViTs in various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. While the individual components are not revolutionary, the unification of token compression methods under a general matrix transformation framework, combined with the practical effectiveness and training-free nature of the approach, constitutes a significant contribution. The strong experimental results across multiple tasks demonstrate the potential impact of this work. However, a deeper theoretical analysis, more extensive ablation studies, and better evaluation of scalability are needed to further strengthen the paper and increase its overall impact. The score reflects the balance between the incremental nature of the components and the novelty and practical significance of the overall framework. The paper's impact could be substantial in making ViTs more efficient, but it will depend on how well the method scales and performs in real-world applications.
