**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of 7 is justified. While the idea of using retrieval to guide image generation isn't completely novel, the specific application to CD-FSOD in a training-free way, the detailed implementation, and the consistent SOTA results warrant a strong score.  The training-free aspect is a major win, but the dependence on the retrieval database and the presence of foreground leakage issues prevent a higher score. The potential for broad adoption and future research directions are promising.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Research on Personalized Financial Product Recommendation by Integrating Large Language Models and Graph Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05873v1)
- **Authors**: Yushang Zhao, Yike Peng, Dannier Li, Yuxin Yang, Chengrui Zhou, Jing Dong
- **Abstract**: With the rapid growth of fintech, personalized financial product recommendations have become increasingly important. Traditional methods like collaborative filtering or content-based models often fail to capture users' latent preferences and complex relationships. We propose a hybrid framework integrating large language models (LLMs) and graph neural networks (GNNs). A pre-trained LLM encodes text data (e.g., user reviews) into rich feature vectors, while a heterogeneous user-product graph models interactions and social ties. Through a tailored message-passing mechanism, text and graph information are fused within the GNN to jointly optimize embeddings. Experiments on public and real-world financial datasets show our model outperforms standalone LLM or GNN in accuracy, recall, and NDCG, with strong interpretability. This work offers new insights for personalized financial recommendations and cross-modal fusion in broader recommendation tasks.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper abstract, introduction, and related sections and will now provide a summary and critical evaluation, including a novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a hybrid framework that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) and Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) for personalized financial product recommendations. The framework consists of three stages: GNN-driven LLM, GNN-LLM co-driven, and LLM-driven GNN. The GNN learns structural information from user-item interaction graphs. This information is then fed into the LLM to enrich semantic representations of users and items. Subsequently, the LLM embeddings enrich the GNN node features in a collaborative fashion. The LLM can also be used to predict graph structures improving GNN node classification or edge prediction performance.  The model is trained using a multi-objective loss function including a pseudo-label-assisted learning component. Experimental results on public and real-world financial datasets demonstrate that the hybrid model outperforms standalone LLM and GNN models in terms of accuracy, recall, and NDCG, while maintaining good interpretability and efficiency. The paper also performs ablation studies to validate the contribution of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Hybrid Approach:** The integration of LLMs and GNNs is a promising approach for recommendation systems, as it leverages the strengths of both models. LLMs capture semantic information from text, while GNNs model complex relationships between users and items.
    *   **Multi-Stage Framework:** The three-stage framework (GNN-driven LLM, GNN-LLM co-driving, and LLM-driven GNN) allows for iterative optimization of both graph structures and text features. This co-driving is a valuable feature.
    *   **Pseudo-Labeling:** The introduction of pseudo-labels as an auxiliary task is a good approach to improve the robustness of the model, especially with sparse or noisy data. This demonstrates a good understanding of the challenges involved in financial product recommendation.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experiments show consistent improvements compared to baselines, indicating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Interpretability Analysis:** The paper attempts to address the "black box" nature of deep learning models by including an interpretability analysis. Gini coefficient interpretation is useful for understand performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of LLMs and GNNs for recommendation is a logical progression, the novelty might be considered somewhat incremental. Other papers have explored similar combinations in different domains. The primary novelty appears to be in the specific architecture, the co-driving stages, and application to the financial domain.
    *   **Dataset Details:** The abstract and initial sections of the paper are sparse on details of the real-world financial dataset used. The number of users, items, interactions, and other relevant statistics are missing, making it difficult to fully assess the significance of the improvements. More details are required about the pre-processing of user reviews etc.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Analysis with State-of-the-Art:** The literature review touches on existing LLM-based and GNN-based recommendation systems, but a more thorough comparison with state-of-the-art methods, particularly hybrid models, is needed. What are other hybrid models for this specific problem?
    *   **Interpretability Limitations:** While the paper mentions interpretability, it is not clear how the Gini Coefficient can be used to interpret how the LLM and GNN contribute to the recommendations. A more detailed explanation or visualization of the interpretability results would be beneficial.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Financial Domain Application:** The application of LLM-GNN hybrid models to financial product recommendation is relevant and timely, given the increasing importance of personalized financial services.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The paper acknowledges practical considerations such as data availability and business needs, making the proposed framework potentially useful in real-world scenarios. The analysis of training time and model size is also useful for practical deployment.
    *   **Multi-Source Heterogeneous Data:** The work attempts to tackle the challenge of integrating multiple sources of heterogeneous data, a common issue in many real-world recommendation problems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and potentially useful approach to personalized financial product recommendation by integrating LLMs and GNNs. The architecture and multi-objective training, along with the experimental results, demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework. However, the level of novelty is somewhat incremental, and the lack of a thorough comparison with state-of-the-art methods and the limited details about the dataset weaken the overall significance. A stronger discussion of the interpretability analysis would also be beneficial.

Considering these strengths and weaknesses, I would rate the paper as:

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Human-AI Alignment of Multimodal Large Language Models with Speech-Language Pathologists in Parent-Child Interactions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05879v1)
- **Authors**: Weiyan Shi, Kenny Tsu Wei Choo
- **Abstract**: Joint attention is a critical marker of early social-communicative development, yet remains difficult for caregivers to assess without expert guidance. In this work, we explore how multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can be aligned with the reasoning processes of speech-language pathologists (SLPs) to support the interpretation of everyday parent-child interactions. We conducted in-depth interviews and video annotation studies with three experienced SLPs to uncover how they evaluate joint attention based on three core behavioural cues: gaze, action, and vocalisation. Using these insights, we developed a two-stage MLLM-based system that first extracts fine-grained behavioural descriptions from video segments and then judge joint attention quality using expert-aligned prompts. Our evaluation across 26 parent-child interaction videos shows that MLLMs can achieve up to 85% accuracy in perceptual cue extraction and over 75% average precision in simulating expert judgement. We further propose design guidelines for building MLLM-based behaviour observation-judgement systems that align with SLPs, emphasising the structuring of behavioural cues, the construction of exemplar libraries grounded in expert annotations, and the need to personalise system responses based on developmental stage and neurotypical or atypical presentation. This work provides structured behavioural cues derived from SLP expertise, demonstrates the feasibility of aligning SLPs observation and judgement using MLLMs, and offers practical design guidelines for building aligned systems to support parent-child interaction analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the alignment of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with the reasoning processes of speech-language pathologists (SLPs) in assessing parent-child interactions, specifically focusing on joint attention. It addresses the lack of access to expert clinical observation for parents in understanding and supporting their child's development. The authors conducted interviews and annotation studies with SLPs to identify key behavioral cues (gaze, action, vocalization) used in evaluating joint attention. They then developed a two-stage MLLM-based system: 1) extracting behavioral descriptions from videos using expert-aligned prompts, and 2) judging joint attention quality based on these descriptions. The system's performance was evaluated on a dataset of parent-child interaction videos, demonstrating promising accuracy in cue extraction and expert judgement simulation. The paper concludes with design guidelines for building MLLM-based behavior observation systems aligned with SLPs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a notable step towards bridging the gap between AI-based assessment of parent-child interactions and expert clinical judgment. While AI in education and family support has been explored, the focus on emulating the specific reasoning processes of SLPs in analyzing complex social behaviors like joint attention is relatively novel. The use of structured behavioral cues derived from SLP expertise to guide MLLM observation and judgement is a valuable contribution.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Real-World Problem:** The paper tackles a significant issue - limited access to SLP services, particularly in underserved areas. The potential for AI to provide initial reflective support to parents, even before formal intervention, is highly relevant.
*   **Structured Approach to a Complex Task:** The breakdown of SLP judgement into gaze, action, and vocalization provides a tangible and structured framework that can be operationalized by AI systems. This simplifies the complex evaluation process, making it easier to model.
*   **Empirical Validation:**  The paper's strength lies in its empirical validation through interviews and annotation studies with experienced SLPs. This grounds the system's design in real-world clinical practice.
*   **MLLM-Based Implementation:** The application of MLLMs to this domain is timely, given their advancements in multimodal understanding. Demonstrating the feasibility of aligning MLLMs with expert observation is significant.
*   **Design Guidelines:** The paper offers practical design guidelines for building similar systems, making it a valuable resource for future research and development.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses only on joint attention and, therefore, has limited generalizability to other aspects of parent-child interaction or different developmental domains.
*   **Dataset Limitations:** The dataset, while curated, is relatively small and primarily consists of videos featuring neurotypical children. This limits the system's ability to handle the complexities of atypical development.
*   **Expert Diversity:** The expert panel comprises three SLPs. A more diverse group would strengthen the findings and reduce potential biases.
*   **MLLM Choices:** The evaluation relied on Gemini Pro 1.5 and the mini versions of GPT-4 which are already outdated as of this assessment.
*   **Overstated Claims:** The title might be seen as somewhat overambitious. While the paper demonstrates alignment, it doesn't necessarily achieve full "human-AI alignment" but rather a simulation of certain aspects of SLP reasoning.
*   **Reasoning Paradox:** While the authors highlighted the un-intuitive nature of the result that not using chain of thought reasoning performed better, they failed to fully explore *why* this happens.

**Score Justification:**

Considering the novelty, significance, and limitations, I assign a score of **7**.

*   **Strengths:** The paper's strength lies in its attempt to bridge expert clinical reasoning and AI. It also identifies and addresses real-world access challenges for parents. The use of structured cues for expert analysis enables the ability to evaluate a difficult modeling problem.
*   **Weaknesses:** The limitations in scope, dataset, and expert diversity temper the score. Also, the design choices may have been outdated because the field advances quickly.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### HMVLM: Multistage Reasoning-Enhanced Vision-Language Model for Long-Tailed Driving Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05883v1)
- **Authors**: Daming Wang, Yuhao Song, Zijian He, Kangliang Chen, Xing Pan, Lu Deng, Weihao Gu
- **Abstract**: We present HaoMo Vision-Language Model (HMVLM), an end-to-end driving framework that implements the slow branch of a cognitively inspired fast-slow architecture. A fast controller outputs low-level steering, throttle, and brake commands, while a slow planner-a large vision-language model-generates high-level intents such as "yield to pedestrian" or "merge after the truck" without compromising latency. HMVLM introduces three upgrades: (1) selective five-view prompting with an embedded 4s history of ego kinematics, (2) multi-stage chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting that enforces a Scene Understanding -> Driving Decision -> Trajectory Inference reasoning flow, and (3) spline-based trajectory post-processing that removes late-stage jitter and sharp turns. Trained on the Waymo Open Dataset, these upgrades enable HMVLM to achieve a Rater Feedback Score (RFS) of 7.7367, securing 2nd place in the 2025 Waymo Vision-based End-to-End (E2E) Driving Challenge and surpassing the public baseline by 2.77%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HMVLM, a vision-language model (VLM) for autonomous driving, designed to handle long-tailed scenarios, meaning infrequent but safety-critical events. HMVLM operates as the "slow" planner within a fast-slow architecture, generating high-level driving intents. The key contributions are: (1) selective multi-view prompting with ego-kinematic history, (2) a multi-stage chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting strategy that enforces Scene Understanding -> Driving Decision -> Trajectory Inference reasoning, and (3) spline-based trajectory smoothing to improve trajectory quality.  The model achieves a strong performance on the Waymo Open Dataset, placing second in the 2025 Vision-based End-to-End Driving Challenge, exceeding the public baseline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The individual components of HMVLM (multi-view prompting, CoT prompting, trajectory smoothing) are not entirely novel in isolation.  However, their *combination* and application within a VLM-based autonomous driving system for explicitly addressing long-tailed scenarios represent a reasonable degree of novelty. The specific structure of the CoT prompting (Scene Understanding -> Driving Decision -> Trajectory Inference) is particularly relevant, given the safety-critical nature of autonomous driving. The adaptation and fine-tuning of a large pre-trained model (Qwen2.5-VL-3B) for this specific task also shows engineering effort, but the method remains similar to previous studies which have employed LLMs for scene understanding and decision making.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in demonstrating the potential of VLMs to tackle the challenge of long-tailed events in autonomous driving.  These events are notoriously difficult for traditional perception and planning systems due to their rarity in training data.  By leveraging the reasoning capabilities of VLMs, the model can potentially generalize to unseen or rare situations. The experimental results, especially the strong performance on the RFS metric in the Waymo Challenge, support this claim. The interpretable intermediate text from the CoT prompting stage, though not a unique feature, also contributes to human-rater trust, a key aspect of deploying autonomous systems. It could reduce the black-box nature of E2E systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition (long-tailed scenarios).
    *   Well-defined architecture and training procedure.
    *   Strong experimental results on a challenging benchmark.
    *   The structured approach (CoT prompting) enhances interpretability.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a large pre-trained model implies high computational cost, which may limit real-world deployment. The paper doesn't sufficiently address this limitation.
    *   The description of the trajectory refinement process, while mentioned, is not detailed enough to fully assess its contribution.
    *   The generalization capability, though implied by the results on long-tailed scenarios, is not explicitly tested with specific out-of-distribution experiments.
    *   The paper only exposes the slow planning system. A complete analysis of the dual fast-slow architecture would enhance the significance of the presented slow planning results.
    *   The novelty is primarily in the integration and application of existing techniques, rather than groundbreaking new methods.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in autonomous driving by promoting the use of VLMs for reasoning and generalization. It can contribute in the area of long-tailed driving scenario decision making using VLMs. But it should be noted that many other works have already integrated LLMs in similar settings.

**Rationale for Score:**

Given the combination of existing techniques into a system tailored for long-tailed events, the solid experimental results, and the potential, albeit not completely groundbreaking, impact on the field, a score of 6 is justified. The limitations related to computational cost, complete architecture integration, and limited demonstration of generalization prevent a higher score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Explainability in Context: A Multilevel Framework Aligning AI Explanations with Stakeholder with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05887v1)
- **Authors**: Marilyn Bello, Rafael Bello, Maria-Matilde García, Ann Nowé, Iván Sevillano-García, Francisco Herrera
- **Abstract**: The growing application of artificial intelligence in sensitive domains has intensified the demand for systems that are not only accurate but also explainable and trustworthy. Although explainable AI (XAI) methods have proliferated, many do not consider the diverse audiences that interact with AI systems: from developers and domain experts to end-users and society. This paper addresses how trust in AI is influenced by the design and delivery of explanations and proposes a multilevel framework that aligns explanations with the epistemic, contextual, and ethical expectations of different stakeholders. The framework consists of three layers: algorithmic and domain-based, human-centered, and social explainability. We highlight the emerging role of Large Language Models (LLMs) in enhancing the social layer by generating accessible, natural language explanations. Through illustrative case studies, we demonstrate how this approach facilitates technical fidelity, user engagement, and societal accountability, reframing XAI as a dynamic, trust-building process.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a multi-level framework for explainable AI (XAI) that aims to align explanations with the diverse epistemic, contextual, and ethical expectations of various stakeholders, ranging from developers and domain experts to end-users and society at large. The framework consists of three layers: (1) algorithmic and domain-based explainability, focusing on fidelity and domain knowledge integration; (2) human-centered explainability, emphasizing interactive dialogues and user-specific adaptation; and (3) social explainability, aiming to ensure societal accountability and trust by tailoring explanations for non-expert users and aligning with ethical and legal standards. The paper highlights the emerging role of Large Language Models (LLMs) in enhancing the social layer by generating accessible, natural language explanations. The authors illustrate the framework's application through illustrative case studies in loan approval scenarios, demonstrating its potential for fostering technical fidelity, user engagement, and societal accountability, ultimately reframing XAI as a dynamic, trust-building process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the integration of LLMs within a structured multi-level XAI framework, particularly with its explicit emphasis on societal explainability. While the individual levels of algorithmic/domain-based and human-centered explainability are not entirely new, the synthesis of these with a dedicated social explainability layer, facilitated by LLMs, is a distinctive contribution. This allows the framework to dynamically produce explanations that are not only technically accurate but also contextually relevant and ethically grounded, a crucial aspect often overlooked in existing XAI approaches.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in current XAI research, which often prioritizes technical transparency without adequately considering the diverse needs and expectations of different stakeholders. The proposed framework offers a structured methodology for tailoring explanations to distinct audiences, ensuring that they are not only technically faithful but also contextually meaningful, ethically grounded, and socially relevant. The integration of LLMs as facilitators of social explainability is particularly significant, as it leverages their natural language generation capabilities to bridge the gap between complex model behavior and human understanding, promoting transparency, trust, and accountability in AI systems.

**Strengths:**
*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The multi-level framework provides a holistic approach to XAI, addressing the needs of diverse stakeholders across the AI lifecycle.
*   **Emphasis on Societal Explainability:** The paper highlights the importance of aligning AI explanations with societal values and ethical considerations, a crucial aspect often overlooked in XAI research.
*   **Integration of LLMs:** The use of LLMs to generate accessible, natural language explanations enhances the usability and interpretability of XAI systems, particularly for non-expert users.
*   **Illustrative Case Studies:** The case studies demonstrate the practical application of the framework in a real-world scenario, showcasing its potential for fostering technical fidelity, user engagement, and societal accountability.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The paper primarily relies on illustrative case studies to demonstrate the framework's effectiveness. Further empirical validation is needed to assess its impact on user trust, understanding, and decision-making across different domains and user groups.
*   **LLM Dependency:** The framework's reliance on LLMs introduces potential challenges related to factual consistency, bias amplification, and explainability of the LLMs themselves, which are not adequately addressed in the paper.
*   **Scalability and Deployment Challenges:** The paper does not discuss the potential scalability and deployment challenges associated with implementing the framework in complex AI systems, particularly in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Metrics for Evaluation:** The paper lacks concrete metrics for evaluating the effectiveness of explanations generated at each level of the framework. Developing such metrics would be beneficial for assessing the framework's performance and identifying areas for improvement.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence XAI research by promoting a more human-centered and socially responsible approach to explanation generation. The multi-level framework offers a valuable roadmap for researchers and practitioners seeking to develop XAI systems that are not only technically sound but also contextually meaningful and ethically aligned. The integration of LLMs as facilitators of social explainability could also inspire further research into the use of natural language generation techniques for bridging the gap between complex model behavior and human understanding.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of XAI by proposing a comprehensive multi-level framework that integrates LLMs for enhanced societal explainability. While the individual components of the framework are not entirely novel, their synthesis and application to real-world scenarios demonstrate a practical approach to fostering transparency, trust, and accountability in AI systems. However, the paper's limited empirical validation, LLM dependency, and lack of concrete metrics for evaluation warrant a more conservative score. A score of 7 reflects the paper's significant potential impact, while also acknowledging its need for further validation and refinement.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### WAKE: Watermarking Audio with Key Enrichment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05891v1)
- **Authors**: Yaoxun Xu, Jianwei Yu, Hangting Chen, Zhiyong Wu, Xixin Wu, Dong Yu, Rongzhi Gu, Yi Luo
- **Abstract**: As deep learning advances in audio generation, challenges in audio security and copyright protection highlight the need for robust audio watermarking. Recent neural network-based methods have made progress but still face three main issues: preventing unauthorized access, decoding initial watermarks after multiple embeddings, and embedding varying lengths of watermarks. To address these issues, we propose WAKE, the first key-controllable audio watermark framework. WAKE embeds watermarks using specific keys and recovers them with corresponding keys, enhancing security by making incorrect key decoding impossible. It also resolves the overwriting issue by allowing watermark decoding after multiple embeddings and supports variable-length watermark insertion. WAKE outperforms existing models in both watermarked audio quality and watermark detection accuracy. Code, more results, and demo page: https://thuhcsi.github.io/WAKE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "WAKE: Watermarking Audio with Key Enrichment":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WAKE, a novel key-controllable audio watermarking framework.  WAKE addresses limitations in existing audio watermarking techniques, specifically focusing on unauthorized watermark extraction, difficulties in decoding after multiple embeddings, and the inability to handle variable-length watermarks. WAKE utilizes key-based embedding and decoding, ensuring that only users with the correct key can extract the watermark. It supports multiple watermarks and enables variable-length watermark insertion. The authors claim that WAKE outperforms existing models in both audio quality and watermark detection accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The concept of key-controllable audio watermarking is a significant advancement. Introducing a key for both embedding and decoding is a natural and necessary evolution in the field, addressing crucial security concerns. The ability to handle multiple watermarks and variable lengths further increases its utility and real-world applicability. While existing watermarking techniques may have considered some of these aspects, WAKE seems to be the first integrated framework addressing all these issues simultaneously. However, the core architecture relies on an invertible neural network (INN), which isn't a novel component in itself but rather a building block adapted for this particular task.

* **Significance:** Addressing the issues of unauthorized access to watermarks and supporting multiple embeddings are vital for real-world deployment of audio watermarking systems. The support for variable-length watermarks adds flexibility, potentially allowing for more complex information to be embedded. The reported improvements in audio quality (SNR/PESQ) and watermark detection accuracy (BER) suggest practical advantages. The significance lies in its potential to enhance audio security and copyright protection in the face of rapidly advancing audio generation technologies.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Key-Controlled Security:** The core strength of WAKE is the introduction of key-based control, making unauthorized watermark extraction practically impossible. This is a crucial feature for protecting copyrighted audio in open environments.
    * **Multi-Watermark Embedding:** The ability to embed and decode multiple watermarks is essential for scenarios where multiple parties need to trace the audio, offering significant advantages over single watermark systems.
    * **Variable Length Support:** Adaptability to various watermark length without the need for retraining significantly improves practicality, and is a useful feature for many applications.
    * **Promising Results:** The authors report compelling experimental results demonstrating superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods in terms of SNR, PESQ, and BER.
    * **Audio Edge Handling:** By introducing BroadWeight constraints, the approach mitigates artifacts at audio edges.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on INN:**  While effective, the core architectural component (INN) is borrowed from existing works. The novelty primarily stems from the intelligent integration of key control and other features.
    * **Audio Editing robustness limitation:** While the model is robust to audio editing operations, these operations are rather basic. An analysis and testing with advanced audio manipulation or adversarial attacks on the watermark could strengthen the contribution.
    * **Lack of analysis on key space:** There's no discussion on the limitations of the key space or potential vulnerabilities related to brute-force key attacks, especially with relatively small key lengths.
    * **Demonstration of practical advantages:** The claims sound promising, but the provided context and justification for them in a real-world application scenario could be improved.

* **Potential Influence:** If the reported performance gains are sustainable across diverse audio datasets and attack scenarios, WAKE could become a widely adopted audio watermarking framework.  The key-controlled aspect is likely to be highly influential, paving the way for more secure and robust watermarking systems. Future works could adopt and build upon this strategy.

* **Justification:** WAKE contributes meaningfully to the field by introducing a novel key-controlled watermarking paradigm with significant practical advantages.  Although its reliance on the INN architecture somewhat reduces its architectural novelty, the integration of various features, performance improvement, and key addition, make it an incremental, rather than revolutionary, advance. It addresses real-world requirements effectively, making it practically valuable. However, the paper could benefit from a stronger emphasis on security analysis and robustness testing against more advanced attacks, as well as a more concrete exposition of its practical application in real-world scenarios.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Route-and-Reason: Scaling Large Language Model Reasoning with Reinforced Model Router
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05901v1)
- **Authors**: Chenyang Shao, Xinyang Liu, Yutang Lin, Fengli Xu, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Multi-step reasoning has proven essential for enhancing the problem-solving capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) by decomposing complex tasks into intermediate steps, either explicitly or implicitly. Extending the reasoning chain at test time through deeper thought processes or broader exploration, can furthur improve performance, but often incurs substantial costs due to the explosion in token usage. Yet, many reasoning steps are relatively simple and can be handled by more efficient smaller-scale language models (SLMs). This motivates hybrid approaches that allocate subtasks across models of varying capacities. However, realizing such collaboration requires accurate task decomposition and difficulty-aware subtask allocation, which is challenging. To address this, we propose R2-Reasoner, a novel framework that enables collaborative reasoning across heterogeneous LLMs by dynamically routing sub-tasks based on estimated complexity. At the core of our framework is a Reinforced Model Router, composed of a task decomposer and a subtask allocator. The task decomposer segments complex input queries into logically ordered subtasks, while the subtask allocator assigns each subtask to the most appropriate model, ranging from lightweight SLMs to powerful LLMs, balancing accuracy and efficiency. To train this router, we introduce a staged pipeline that combines supervised fine-tuning on task-specific datasets with Group Relative Policy Optimization algorithm, enabling self-supervised refinement through iterative reinforcement learning. Extensive experiments across four challenging benchmarks demonstrate that R2-Reasoner reduces API costs by 86.85% while maintaining or surpassing baseline accuracy. Our framework paves the way for more cost-effective and adaptive LLM reasoning. The code is open-source at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/R2_Reasoner .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Route-and-Reason: Scaling Large Language Model Reasoning with Reinforced Model Router" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R2-Reasoner, a framework designed to improve the efficiency of large language model (LLM) reasoning by dynamically routing sub-tasks to the most appropriate model based on estimated complexity. R2-Reasoner uses a Reinforced Model Router, which includes a task decomposer and a subtask allocator. The task decomposer breaks down complex queries into logically ordered subtasks, while the subtask allocator assigns each subtask to a model ranging from smaller, more efficient language models (SLMs) to more powerful LLMs. The router is trained using a staged pipeline combining supervised fine-tuning with a Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) reinforcement learning algorithm. The experimental results on various benchmarks demonstrate that R2-Reasoner can reduce API costs while maintaining or improving accuracy compared to baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically routing sub-tasks to models of varying capacity isn't entirely novel. Existing methods (e.g., DataShunt) have explored similar concepts. However, the paper's novelty lies in the specific implementation of the Reinforced Model Router, the staged training pipeline using GRPO for self-supervised refinement, and the combination of a task decomposer and subtask allocator working in concert. The emphasis on balancing accuracy and efficiency with a reinforcement learning approach also contributes to the novelty. The task decomposition component's evaluation metrics and data generation process appear well-considered.

*   **Significance:** The problem the paper addresses – the high cost of LLM reasoning – is highly significant. The growing trend of test-time scaling makes reasoning capabilities more accessible but also presents a practical barrier due to increased computational demands.  A framework that significantly reduces API costs while maintaining or improving accuracy has the potential to make advanced LLM reasoning techniques more widely accessible and practical. The reported 86.85% cost reduction is particularly impressive. The open-source availability of the code will further encourage adoption and build upon the contributions of this paper.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a critical and practical problem in LLM deployment.
    *   The proposed R2-Reasoner framework is well-structured and clearly described.
    *   The use of a staged training pipeline with GRPO is a solid approach to optimizing the router.
    *   The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating significant cost reductions with comparable or improved accuracy.
    *   The ablation studies showing the impact of SFT and RL are helpful in understanding the framework's behavior.
    *   The code is publicly available, increasing reproducibility and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the results are positive, it's crucial to consider potential limitations related to the specific benchmarks used.  The robustness of the framework should be further validated on a broader range of tasks and datasets.
    *   The evaluation could include a more detailed analysis of the types of tasks where the R2-Reasoner excels and those where it might struggle.  Understanding these limitations is crucial for practical deployment.
    *   There is an assumption that an array of models, large and small, are already deployed and running at the edge. It may be useful to provide some context as to how to select a diverse model set.
    *   The sensitivity of performance to hyperparameters, especially in the GRPO training, could be discussed more explicitly.
    *   The description of the procedural review mechanism could be more detailed, including examples.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence research and development in the areas of LLM efficiency, resource-aware reasoning, and hierarchical model collaboration. The idea of dynamically routing tasks based on complexity could inspire other researchers to explore similar approaches in different contexts. The successful application of GRPO to optimize the model router could also encourage its wider adoption in other LLM training tasks. If the framework proves robust and scalable, it could have a significant impact on the way LLMs are deployed and utilized in real-world applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the non-trivial novelty in the approach (staged training, GRPO use, dual components) and its significance in addressing a critical problem (LLM cost), along with the impressive experimental results, I would assign a score of 7. The availability of code enhances the score. While the core idea of model routing has been explored before, the specific implementation and the thorough evaluation merit recognition. The limitations relating to generalization across different benchmarks and potential sensitivity to hyperparameters temper this score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generating Grounded Responses to Counter Misinformation via Learning Efficient Fine-Grained Critiques
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05924v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaofei Xu, Xiuzhen Zhang, Ke Deng
- **Abstract**: Fake news and misinformation poses a significant threat to society, making efficient mitigation essential. However, manual fact-checking is costly and lacks scalability. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer promise in automating counter-response generation to mitigate misinformation, but a critical challenge lies in their tendency to hallucinate non-factual information. Existing models mainly rely on LLM self-feedback to reduce hallucination, but this approach is computationally expensive. In this paper, we propose MisMitiFact, Misinformation Mitigation grounded in Facts, an efficient framework for generating fact-grounded counter-responses at scale. MisMitiFact generates simple critique feedback to refine LLM outputs, ensuring responses are grounded in evidence. We develop lightweight, fine-grained critique models trained on data sourced from readily available fact-checking sites to identify and correct errors in key elements such as numerals, entities, and topics in LLM generations. Experiments show that MisMitiFact generates counter-responses of comparable quality to LLMs' self-feedback while using significantly smaller critique models. Importantly, it achieves ~5x increase in feedback generation throughput, making it highly suitable for cost-effective, large-scale misinformation mitigation. Code and LLM prompt templates are at https://github.com/xxfwin/MisMitiFact.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Generating Grounded Responses to Counter Misinformation via Learning Efficient Fine-Grained Critiques":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MisMitiFact, a framework designed to generate fact-grounded counter-responses to misinformation using Large Language Models (LLMs). The key idea is to use lightweight, fine-grained critique models to provide feedback on an LLM's initial response, specifically targeting elements like numbers, entities, and topics. These critique models are trained on data derived from readily available fact-checking sources, avoiding the need for expensive human annotations or repeated LLM calls for self-feedback. The framework aims to improve the factuality of LLM-generated counter-responses while maintaining efficiency, making it suitable for large-scale misinformation mitigation. Experiments on real-world datasets (PUBHEALTH and COVID-19 Vaccine) demonstrate comparable quality to LLM self-feedback approaches, but with significantly reduced computational cost and increased throughput.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Fine-grained Critique:** The core novelty lies in the use of specialized, lightweight critique models focusing on specific aspects of factuality (numbers, entities, topics). This contrasts with general-purpose critique or self-feedback approaches that are computationally more expensive.
*   **Automated Training Data:**  The paper leverages readily available fact-checking articles to automatically generate training data for the critique models. This avoids the need for human-annotated critiques, which is a significant advantage.
*   **System-level Integration:** While individual components (LLMs, fact-checking datasets) are not entirely novel, the integration of these components into a specific pipeline with fine-grained critique for misinformation mitigation is a novel contribution.

**Significance:**

*   **Practical Application:** The paper addresses a crucial problem of misinformation mitigation, which has significant societal impact.  The proposed approach is designed to be efficient and scalable, making it practically relevant.
*   **Efficiency:** The ~5x throughput improvement compared to LLM self-feedback is a compelling result, showcasing the potential for cost-effective large-scale deployment.
*   **Reduced Hallucination:** The focus on fact-grounded responses is a direct attempt to address the well-known issue of LLM hallucination, which is essential for trustworthy misinformation mitigation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of generating factually accurate counter-responses and articulates the desired properties of such responses.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** The MisMitiFact framework is well-structured and the individual components (critique models, response generation) are clearly explained.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on two real-world datasets, comparing MisMitiFact to several strong baselines.  The evaluation metrics are well-chosen and appropriate.
*   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the MisMitiFact framework.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Critiques:** While targeting numbers, entities, and topics is a good starting point, it doesn't cover all aspects of factuality.  More complex reasoning errors or nuanced misinterpretations might not be caught by these critiques.
*   **Reliance on Fact-Checking Data Quality:** The critique models are trained on data from fact-checking sites.  The performance of the system is therefore heavily reliant on the quality and comprehensiveness of this data.  Potential biases or limitations in the fact-checking data could propagate into the counter-responses.
*   **Simplistic Critique Generation:** The critique templates ("X is not correct, the correct number is Y") are very basic.  More sophisticated critique generation methods could potentially lead to further improvements.
*   **Generalizability concerns:** The COVID-19 dataset is small (307 samples). Experiments conducted on more diverse and larger datasets are required to confirm the generalizability of MisMitiFact.
*   **Evaluation limitations:** Human evaluation of generated text can be very expensive. Using GPT-4 to evaluate other LLM outputs introduces potential bias, though it may be a cost-effective substitute for direct human evaluation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed approach to generating fact-grounded counter-responses to misinformation. The novelty lies in the efficient fine-grained critique framework, which offers a practical alternative to computationally expensive self-feedback methods. The empirical results demonstrate the potential for improved efficiency without sacrificing quality. However, the limitations regarding the scope of critiques, reliance on fact-checking data quality, and simplicity of critique generation prevent a higher score. The limitations also raise questions about generalizability, especially on datasets beyond the two explored in the paper.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Small Models, Big Support: A Local LLM Framework for Teacher-Centric Content Creation and Assessment using RAG and CAG
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05925v1)
- **Authors**: Zarreen Reza, Alexander Mazur, Michael T. Dugdale, Robin Ray-Chaudhuri
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly utilized as student-facing educational aids, their potential to directly support educators, particularly through locally deployable and customizable open-source solutions, remains significantly underexplored. Many existing educational solutions rely on cloud-based infrastructure or proprietary tools, which are costly and may raise privacy concerns. Regulated industries with limited budgets require affordable, self-hosted solutions. We introduce an end-to-end, open-source framework leveraging small (3B-7B parameters), locally deployed LLMs for customized teaching material generation and assessment. Our system uniquely incorporates an interactive loop crucial for effective small-model refinement, and an auxiliary LLM verifier to mitigate jailbreaking risks, enhancing output reliability and safety. Utilizing Retrieval and Context Augmented Generation (RAG/CAG), it produces factually accurate, customized pedagogically-styled content. Deployed on-premises for data privacy and validated through an evaluation pipeline and a college physics pilot, our findings show that carefully engineered small LLM systems can offer robust, affordable, practical, and safe educator support, achieving utility comparable to larger models for targeted tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework for supporting educators using small, locally deployed Large Language Models (LLMs). The system focuses on teacher-centric tasks like generating customized educational content, creating grading rubrics, and assisting with student assessment. The framework uses Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) and Context Augmented Generation (CAG) to enhance the LLM's capabilities and includes an interactive refinement loop for iterative feedback. A unique feature is the inclusion of an auxiliary LLM verifier to mitigate jailbreaking and hallucination risks, improving output reliability and safety. The system prioritizes data privacy and institutional sovereignty by operating entirely locally. The authors present an evaluation methodology, experimental results, and insights from a pilot deployment in a college physics course, highlighting the potential of small LLMs for effective and affordable educator support.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Focus on Educator Support:** The paper addresses a crucial gap by focusing on tools for educators rather than solely student-facing applications, a significant and often overlooked area in AI in education.
    * **Emphasis on Local Deployment and Data Privacy:** The framework's design around small, open-weights LLMs and local deployment directly tackles concerns about cost, data privacy, and institutional sovereignty, making it relevant for resource-constrained and regulated environments. This is a pragmatic and important consideration.
    * **Novel Architectural Components:** The inclusion of the interactive refinement loop and the verifier LLM addresses critical limitations of smaller LLMs, enhancing their utility and reliability.  The verifier, in particular, is a valuable addition that directly confronts a key challenge of using smaller models: their increased vulnerability to problematic inputs and outputs.
    * **Synergistic Use of RAG and CAG:** The thoughtful combination of RAG and CAG to tailor content generation and factual grounding is a strong point, demonstrating an understanding of the complementary strengths of these techniques.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation Methodology:** The paper employs both quantitative (ROUGE, BLEU, METEOR, BERTScore) and qualitative (LLM-based) evaluation metrics to assess the framework's performance.
    * **Pilot Study:** The deployment in a real-world college physics course adds practical value, validating the system's feasibility and uncovering challenges for future development.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the physics course pilot is valuable, the evaluation remains limited in scope.  Testing across multiple disciplines and with a larger cohort of educators would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.  The number of lab exercises generated is relatively small.
    * **Reliance on Reference Materials:** The quantitative evaluation relies on reference materials (e.g., teacher-authored examples), which may limit the ability to assess the framework's creativity or explore novel pedagogical approaches. The need to generate high-quality reference material is a barrier to scale.
    * **Complexity of Student Submission Analyzer:** While promising, the multi-stage pipeline for student submission analysis is quite complex. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of its limitations, potential failure points, and scalability challenges. A more rigorous error analysis and comparison against alternative methods is desirable.
    * **Modest Improvements of smaller models** The results indicate modest improvements, not necessarily large improvements, with some of the smaller models.
* **Novelty and Significance:**
    * The paper carves a niche by focusing on teacher-centric AI tools designed for local deployment and data privacy. The emphasis on open-weights, smaller models is a practical response to the high cost and regulatory challenges associated with large, cloud-based LLMs. The combination of RAG/CAG, the interactive refinement loop, and the verifier LLM demonstrates a novel approach to enhancing the utility and safety of smaller LLMs.
    * The work contributes to the growing body of research on AI in education, addressing a critical need for affordable, customizable, and secure tools that empower educators.

* **Overall Impression** The paper presents a well-motivated and designed framework for leveraging smaller LLMs to support educators. The focus on local deployment, data privacy, and a teacher-centric approach are particularly valuable.  However, the limitations in the evaluation scope and some complexity within the architecture hold the paper back from achieving a higher score. The potential impact is real but requires further validation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's notable contributions in terms of addressing a crucial gap (teacher-centric AI), practical design choices (local deployment, small models), and novel architectural elements (verifier LLM, interactive refinement). The emphasis on addressing practical challenges rather than simply achieving state-of-the-art performance pushes it beyond a purely incremental contribution. However, the limited scope of evaluation and the complexity of the Student Submission Analyzer justify withholding a higher score. Further work across disciplines and with more robust evaluation methodologies will be necessary to demonstrate the full potential of the framework and support a higher score in the future.

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoA: Heterogeneous Mixture of Adapters for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05928v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Cao, Tianwei Lin, Hongyang He, Rolan Yan, Wenqiao Zhang, Juncheng Li, Dongping Zhang, Siliang Tang, Yueting Zhuang
- **Abstract**: Recent studies integrate Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) and Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) to further enhance the performance of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods in Large Language Model (LLM) applications. Existing methods employ \emph{homogeneous} MoE-LoRA architectures composed of LoRA experts with either similar or identical structures and capacities. However, these approaches often suffer from representation collapse and expert load imbalance, which negatively impact the potential of LLMs. To address these challenges, we propose a \emph{heterogeneous} \textbf{Mixture-of-Adapters (MoA)} approach. This method dynamically integrates PEFT adapter experts with diverse structures, leveraging their complementary representational capabilities to foster expert specialization, thereby enhancing the effective transfer of pre-trained knowledge to downstream tasks. MoA supports two variants: \textbf{(i)} \textit{Soft MoA} achieves fine-grained integration by performing a weighted fusion of all expert outputs; \textbf{(ii)} \textit{Sparse MoA} activates adapter experts sparsely based on their contribution, achieving this with negligible performance degradation. Experimental results demonstrate that heterogeneous MoA outperforms homogeneous MoE-LoRA methods in both performance and parameter efficiency. Our project is available at https://github.com/DCDmllm/MoA.
- **Summary**: The paper "MoA: Heterogeneous Mixture of Adapters for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models" introduces a novel Mixture-of-Adapters (MoA) approach for parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) of large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of existing MoE-LoRA methods, which often suffer from representation collapse and expert load imbalance due to their homogeneous architectures. MoA dynamically integrates PEFT adapter experts with diverse structures, leveraging their complementary representational capabilities to foster expert specialization. The paper proposes two variants: Soft MoA, which performs a weighted fusion of all expert outputs, and Sparse MoA, which activates experts sparsely based on their contribution, achieving comparable performance with improved parameter efficiency. The experimental results demonstrate that heterogeneous MoA outperforms homogeneous MoE-LORA methods in both performance and parameter efficiency across various tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using *heterogeneous* adapters in a MoE setting for PEFT is a significant contribution. Prior work largely focused on homogeneous adapter mixtures. By leveraging different adapter architectures (LoRA, Parallel Adapters, Prompt Tuning) within the same MoE framework, the paper introduces a more nuanced and potentially more effective way to combine PEFT techniques. The *dynamic* expert activation through the sparse routing mechanism is also a valuable innovation. The introduction of SoftMoA and SparseMoA variants further enhances the paper's originality.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in PEFT: the saturation of representational capacity in LoRA and the redundancy/load imbalance issues in homogeneous MoE-LoRA approaches. MoA offers a promising solution by promoting expert specialization and reducing computational overhead. The experimental results showing performance gains and improved parameter efficiency compared to existing methods are significant and suggest the potential for real-world impact. The improvements in training and inference efficiency are also valuable for practical deployment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem definition is well-motivated and clearly articulated.
    *   The proposed MoA approach is technically sound and well-explained.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough and comprehensive, covering a range of benchmarks and baseline methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   The in-depth analysis provides clear insights into the router weight distribution, expert specialization and computational efficiency gains.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates significant improvements, a more detailed analysis of specific downstream tasks could illustrate the relative advantages of *Soft MoA* versus *Sparse MoA*, and how their architectural nuances affect performance on various tasks.
    *   While the paper compares performance, the specific trade-offs between parameter counts, GPU memory footprint and actual deployment costs could be enhanced with a more practical and "real-world" deployment analysis to make more informed decisions on their architecture.
    *   The limitations section could explore how MoA could be integrated into other model types to further push the boundaries of the work and promote integration with other PEFT methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of PEFT by providing a more flexible and efficient approach to fine-tuning LLMs. The MoA framework could be adopted and extended by other researchers and practitioners. The insights into expert specialization and dynamic routing could inspire new approaches to MoE architectures.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to PEFT. The use of heterogeneous adapters in a MoE setting is a smart solution to the limitations of homogeneous architectures. The comprehensive experimental evaluation and ablation studies provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While there are some weaknesses, the strengths of the paper outweigh them.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### FADE: Frequency-Aware Diffusion Model Factorization for Video Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05934v1)
- **Authors**: Yixuan Zhu, Haolin Wang, Shilin Ma, Wenliang Zhao, Yansong Tang, Lei Chen, Jie Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in diffusion frameworks have significantly enhanced video editing, achieving high fidelity and strong alignment with textual prompts. However, conventional approaches using image diffusion models fall short in handling video dynamics, particularly for challenging temporal edits like motion adjustments. While current video diffusion models produce high-quality results, adapting them for efficient editing remains difficult due to the heavy computational demands that prevent the direct application of previous image editing techniques. To overcome these limitations, we introduce FADE, a training-free yet highly effective video editing approach that fully leverages the inherent priors from pre-trained video diffusion models via frequency-aware factorization. Rather than simply using these models, we first analyze the attention patterns within the video model to reveal how video priors are distributed across different components. Building on these insights, we propose a factorization strategy to optimize each component's specialized role. Furthermore, we devise spectrum-guided modulation to refine the sampling trajectory with frequency domain cues, preventing information leakage and supporting efficient, versatile edits while preserving the basic spatial and temporal structure. Extensive experiments on real-world videos demonstrate that our method consistently delivers high-quality, realistic and temporally coherent editing results both qualitatively and quantitatively. Code is available at https://github.com/EternalEvan/FADE .
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FADE: Frequency-Aware Diffusion Model Factorization for Video Editing."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FADE, a training-free video editing approach that leverages pre-trained text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models. The method hinges on a frequency-aware factorization strategy, exploiting the observation that different layers (blocks) within T2V models contribute differently to video generation: early blocks capture foundational spatial and temporal structures (low frequencies), while later blocks refine high-frequency details. FADE factorizes the editing process by focusing on the sketching blocks for structural reconstruction and using spectrum-guided modulation, based on 3D Fourier transforms, to guide the sampling trajectory. This approach aims to preserve unedited content (low frequencies) while enabling flexible edits (high frequencies) by modulating the sampling process with the sketching blocks' full-attention outputs. FADE demonstrates the ability to perform diverse edits (appearance and motion) while maintaining high fidelity and temporal consistency without requiring fine-tuning or optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the *frequency-aware factorization* approach to video editing within a diffusion model context. While the observation that different layers in neural networks capture different levels of detail isn't entirely new, its application to video editing, particularly in the way FADE leverages the sketching blocks and 3D Fourier transform, is a significant contribution. Specifically, the choice of using only early layers instead of all layers/tuning helps both efficiency and results. This allows the model to be both efficient and performant. Prior methods have focused on attention injection or fine-tuning, which are computationally expensive. The paper builds on existing knowledge of attention maps within diffusion models but refines them into a method more applicable for video edits.

*   **Significance:**  The method offers improvements in terms of computational efficiency and editing flexibility compared to prior methods. The training-free nature of FADE is a significant advantage, making it more accessible and practical. The ability to handle both appearance and motion adjustments within a single framework also increases its applicability. The quantitative results demonstrate improvements in textual alignment and temporal consistency (CLIP score and OSV metrics). The qualitative results further support the claim of improved realism and coherence, especially for motion edits. The method could have broad impact due to its efficiency in an area with historically high compute demands. Further, the code is available, meaning more people can adopt and expand upon the method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Computationally efficient compared to fine-tuning or optimization-based methods.
    *   Training-free.
    *   Handles both appearance and motion edits.
    *   Achieves high-fidelity and temporally consistent results.
    *   The factorization strategy is well-motivated and effectively leverages the inherent priors of pre-trained T2V models.
    *   The use of frequency-domain cues for modulation helps balance reconstruction and editing.
    *   Good quantitative and qualitative results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The improvement compared to the current state-of-the-art approaches appears incremental, not revolutionary. Although the method consistently gets better results, there is no step change compared to fine-tuning methods.
    *   Relies on the quality of the underlying T2V model; limitations of the base model (occlusion, temporal reasoning) will affect FADE's performance.
    *   The paper is well written but could benefit from more extensive qualitative comparisons, especially showcasing failure cases and a deeper analysis of the limitations.

*   **Potential Influence:** FADE has the potential to influence future research on video editing by providing a more efficient and accessible approach to leveraging pre-trained diffusion models. The frequency-aware factorization strategy could be adopted and adapted for other video editing tasks and models.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper is strong, and the frequency aware factorization gives it a significant improvement in both efficiency and results. However, the results are only incrementally better. The improvements are well-motivated, executed, and evaluated. While the approach isn't groundbreaking, it provides a practical and valuable contribution. Therefore, it deserves a solid score reflecting its utility and potential impact within the video editing research community.
